# The Lean index

What we have proved -- the source of truth (Kaveh 2026-10-10: check here before building anything; prove before
relying). Generated by `python -m catspace.incubator.analyst.lean_index build`; search with `... lean_index search <words>`.
Do not edit by hand.

977 theorems/lemmas (179 OPEN -- still `sorry`) and 756 documented definitions (the kernel's decision procedures) in 91 files.

## lean/ideas/Ideas/Core/Board.lean

- **def `Ideas.Core.IsShortest`** (line 41): `n` is the length of a SHORTEST walk from `s` to `t` in `step`.
- **def `Ideas.Core.pathDist`** (line 45): The length of a shortest walk from `s` to `t` (0 when `t` cannot be reached: use it with `pathDist_step`'s reachability hypothesis, or on a connected step relation).
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.pathDist_isShortest`** (line 48)
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.pathDist_self`** (line 55)
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.pathDist_step`** (line 62): THE LEMMA OF 12.2: the shortest path in a step relation changes by at most one per step
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.pathDist_lipschitz`** (line 69): On a step relation that connects every pair of places, the shortest path is a distance: the hypothesis `dist_step` of the board laws holds of it.
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.pathDist_step_symm`** (line 74): If steps run both ways (a symmetric relation), the shortest path changes by at most one in EITHER direction.
- **structure `Ideas.Core.Board`** (line 84): THE DATA a game supplies for the frame and path results: a `Game` over `State` and `Move` (`toGame`), places, units, sides, kinds.
- **structure `Ideas.Core.Board.Laws`** (line 113): THE FIVE PROPERTIES, each once
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.Board.far_stays_off`** (line 144): `far_stays_off` for a kind of the board: a unit of kind `k` more than `K` moves from every place of `B` is, after `j ≤ K` steps, still more than `K - j` from each.
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/race.py
- **theorem `Ideas.Core.Board.far_not_bearing`** (line 149): `far_not_bearing` for a kind of the board: it stands on no place of `B` during its `K` tempi.

## lean/ideas/Ideas/GeoLemmas.lean

- **def `Ideas.GeoLemmas.colour`** (line 23): The colour of a square: (file + rank) mod 2.
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/lemmas/ontology/file_parity.json, catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/lemmas/ontology/rank_parity.json
- **def `Ideas.GeoLemmas.orthAdj`** (line 26): Two squares are orthogonal neighbours.
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.absDiff_def`** (line 28)
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.knight_flips_colour`** (line 31): STAGE 1, the knight: every knight move changes the colour of the square.
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.bishop_keeps_colour`** (line 38): STAGE 1, the bishop: every bishop move keeps the colour of the square.
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/lemma_explorer.py, rust/analyst_core/src/plan.rs
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.orthAdj_other_colour`** (line 45): Orthogonal neighbours are of opposite colour.
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.knight_hop_never_covers_orth_neighbour`** (line 51): STAGE 2, Kaveh's lemma: a knight that hops from `s` to `t` never attacks an orthogonal neighbour of `s` (the knight on d7 cannot hop and still guard d8).
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.knight_drops_every_guard`** (line 64): STAGE 2: a knight that moves stops guarding EVERY square it guarded -- from `s` it attacks only the colour opposite to `s`, from `t` only the colour of `s`.
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.knight_leaves_every_line`** (line 77): STAGE 2: a knight move leaves the file, the rank and both diagonals of its square -- so a knight standing on any line through `s` always opens it (a pinned knight is frozen: no move of it keeps the line blocked).
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/lemma_explorer.py, rust/analyst_core/src/plan.rs
- **theorem `Ideas.GeoLemmas.knight_fork_same_colour`** (line 88): STAGE 2: a knight forks only two squares of the same colour -- two pieces on opposite colours cannot be forked.
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/agenda.py, catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/landing.py

## lean/ideas/Ideas/InsufficientMaterial.lean

- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.kg_iff`** (line 29): A king's step, in file and rank numbers.
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.orth_iff`** (line 36): Orthogonal neighbours, in file and rank numbers.
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.king_guards_two_orth`** (line 42): A king that guards two different orthogonal neighbours of `k` stands next to `k` (or on it).
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.two_orth`** (line 54): Every square has two different orthogonal neighbours on the board.
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.minor_colour`** (line 68): THE MINOR PIECE'S COLOUR CLASS: a knight or a bishop on `m` that attacks `k` attacks only squares of `k`'s colour.
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.minor_attack`** (line 81): An attack by a minor piece of kind `r` standing on `m` is its geometry.
- **def `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.kmk`** (line 90): Exactly three pieces: the side to move has only its king, on `k`; the other side a king on `w` and one minor piece of kind `r` (a knight or a bishop) on `m`.
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.no_mate_king_minor`** (line 95): A KING AND ONE MINOR PIECE NEVER MATE a lone king (the kings apart, as they always are).
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/lemma_explorer.py
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.no_mate_by_lone_king`** (line 171): A LONE KING NEVER CHECKS: when the other side has nothing but a king on `w`, not next to ours on `k`, the side to move is not mated
- **theorem `Ideas.InsufficientMaterial.insufficient_material`** (line 188): INSUFFICIENT MATERIAL, the static rule: two kings apart and at most one more piece, a knight or a bishop of either side -- no checkmate.
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/lemma_explorer.py, rust/analyst_core/src/game.rs

## lean/ideas/Ideas/Landing.lean

- **def `Ideas.Landing.land`** (line 24): THE LANDING: `pc` lifted from `frm`, placed on `to`, the side other than `c` to move (no en-passant square, as a counterfactual has no history).
- **theorem `Ideas.Landing.land_board_to`** (line 28): The piece stands on its landing square.
- **theorem `Ideas.Landing.land_turn`** (line 32): THEM to move.
- **theorem `Ideas.Landing.land_board_other`** (line 35): Nothing else moved: every square but the two keeps its piece.
- **theorem `Ideas.Landing.land_agree`** (line 41): THE KEY OF THE TABLE: two positions that agree on a set of squares give landings that agree on it and on the two squares of the landing -- the answer to a question that reads only those squares needs nothing else to be r
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/landing.py
- **def `Ideas.Landing.Target`** (line 58): A TARGET of `c`: a claim binds in the landing (the claim is a statement about a position: a mate, an exchange won, a fork, a promotion by the passer lemma)
- **theorem `Ideas.Landing.target_of_checkmate`** (line 62): A landing that mates is a target.

## lean/ideas/Ideas/Mate.lean

- **def `Ideas.withEmpty`** (line 26): `q` with square `s` emptied: the defender's king lifted off the board, so squares it could flee to are judged with the attacker's lines running through its old square.
- **def `Ideas.kingOnlyAtB`** (line 30): The colour-`c` king stands on `k` and nowhere else.
- **def `Ideas.flightOkB`** (line 35): The square `d` is no flight for the king on `k`: not a king step, or held by the defender's own piece, or attacked by the attacker with the king lifted off the board.
- **def `Ideas.flightsCoveredB`** (line 40): No square next to the king is a flight.
- **def `Ideas.checksB`** (line 44): The attacker's piece on `c` attacks the king on `k`.
- **def `Ideas.noCaptureB`** (line 48): No defender's piece but the king attacks `c` (pins ignored: an over-approximation).
- **def `Ideas.couldReachEmptyB`** (line 53): The defender's piece on `s` could step onto the empty square `u`: a pawn by a single or double push (path not checked), any other piece but the king along its attack lines.
- **def `Ideas.noBlockB`** (line 63): No defender's piece can step onto a square between `c` and `k`.
- **theorem `Ideas.kingOnlyAtB_iff`** (line 68): No defender's piece can step onto a square between `c` and `k`
- **theorem `Ideas.kingSquare_of_only`** (line 72)
- **theorem `Ideas.pieceAttacksB_ne`** (line 84)
- **theorem `Ideas.pieceAttacksB_source`** (line 94)
- **theorem `Ideas.clearBetweenB_mono`** (line 103)
- **theorem `Ideas.pieceAttacksB_mono`** (line 114): An attack survives any change of the board that keeps the attacker on its square and keeps empty every square between that was empty.
- **theorem `Ideas.capture_geom_attacks`** (line 138): An attack survives any change of the board that keeps the attacker on its square and keeps empty every square between that was empty
- **theorem `Ideas.empty_geom_reaches`** (line 149)
- **theorem `Ideas.not_noBlock`** (line 161)
- **theorem `Ideas.not_noCapture`** (line 168)
- **theorem `Ideas.attacked_of_checks`** (line 173)
- **theorem `Ideas.checker_ne_defender`** (line 179): The checker's colour is not the defender's.
- **theorem `Ideas.king_step_in_check`** (line 190): A king step leaves the king in check: every flight is held or covered.
- **theorem `Ideas.other_move_in_check`** (line 235): Any other ordinary move leaves the king in check: it cannot take the checker, cannot step between, and does not move the king.
- **theorem `Ideas.promotion_in_check`** (line 302): A promotion leaves the king in check: the pawn cannot take the checker or step between.
- **theorem `Ideas.castle_source_king`** (line 376): A castling move starts from its king square.
- **theorem `Ideas.castle_turn`** (line 386)
- **theorem `Ideas.castle_illegal`** (line 394): Castling is never legal out of check.
- **theorem `Ideas.checkmate_of_unmeetable_check`** (line 414): **A check that cannot be met is mate.** If the defender's king stands alone on `k`, the attacker's piece on `c` checks it, en passant is off, no square next to the king is a flight, no defender's piece but the king attac
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/regress.py, catspace/incubator/analyst/lean_ideas.py, catspace/incubator/analyst/lemma_explorer.py

- **def `Ideas.Passer.kdist`** (line 59): King distance (Chebyshev).
- **def `Ideas.Passer.push`** (line 62): Our next pawn move from `s`: a push, or the promotion to a queen from the seventh rank.

- **def `Ideas.Passer.kingHasMoveB`** (line 107): After our first push, the enemy king has an empty neighbour that we do not attack.
- **def `Ideas.Passer.targets`** (line 128): Where to look when one hypothesis fails.

- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.bare_king_move`** (line 179): When the side to move has only its king, on `ek`, a legal move is a king step to a square free of its own pieces that leaves the king unattacked.

- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.ahead_one_vals`** (line 289): The coordinates one step ahead.

- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.self_not_mem_path`** (line 340)

- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.kdist_of_mem_path`** (line 357): A square on the path is nearer the promotion square than the pawn.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.pushes_ahead`** (line 368): One step ahead is one push nearer.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.ahead_mem_path`** (line 375)

- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.bare_attack`** (line 455): With one enemy piece, its king on `ek`, "attacked by the enemy" is "next to `ek`".
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.noslider_attack`** (line 481): Attacks by a side without sliders do not depend on the rest of the board: only the attacker's square and kind matter.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.push_eq`** (line 516): Our pawn's next move, with the square it goes to.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.push_legal`** (line 521): Our push exists and is legal.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.push_board_two`** (line 617): What our push does to the board, with two or more pushes to go: the pawn steps to `y`.

- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.push_keeps_enemy`** (line 645): The enemy's king and pieces are untouched by our push.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.enemy_has_move`** (line 667): After our push (more than one to go) the enemy has a legal move: the king steps to the free square.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.noslider_pieceAttacks`** (line 741): A piece of a side without sliders attacks by geometry alone: the board around it does not matter.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.king_attack`** (line 752): A king attacks by king geometry.
- **theorem `Ideas.Passer.pawn_attack`** (line 759): A pawn attacks by pawn geometry.

- **theorem `Ideas.Region.no_rook_mate_in_open`** (line 107): THE ROOK ALONE, IN THE OPEN: with the kings apart (always, on a board), our king and a rook never cover the whole 3x3 box of the lone king on (ka, kb) -- the lone king, off the edge, is never mated by a king and a rook
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/region.py
- **def `Ideas.Region.chebLe`** (line 115): Kings are within `d` of each other (Chebyshev distance at most `d`).
- **theorem `Ideas.Region.evasion_step`** (line 121): THE MEASURE THEY HOLD (a king never gains on a king with room): our king at distance two or more from theirs makes one king move (to (wa', wb')); then one of the four orthogonal steps of their king, the one away along th
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/region.py

## lean/ideas/Ideas/Root.lean

- **theorem `Ideas.Root.sum_two_changed`** (line 32)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.material_two_changed`** (line 63): Two positions whose boards agree off `s`, `t`: their material differs by what the two squares hold.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.material_boardMove`** (line 77): ITEM 1 (a, b together): a piece moves from `s` to `t`; each side's material afterwards plus what that side had on `t` is what it was before
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.contrib_other`** (line 91)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.contrib_own`** (line 95)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.contrib_none`** (line 99)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.worthOf_eq`** (line 102)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.capture_gains`** (line 109): ITEM 2: a normal move of the side to move onto a piece of the other side: its material is unchanged, the other side's falls by `worthOf p y` (stated as the two equations, so no truncated subtraction hides in them).
  - used by: catspace/incubator/analyst/discovery/selfmine.py
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.capture_balance`** (line 124): The same in the inequality form the root uses, in the balance: after the capture our balance is at least what it was plus the worth.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.material_boardMove_empty`** (line 134): A piece moves onto an empty square: nobody's material changes.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.castle_material`** (line 141): Castling moves a king and a rook onto empty squares: nobody's material changes.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.normal_capture_attacked`** (line 183): What lands on a piece attacks it: a normal capture's geometry is the attack.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.legal_pseudo`** (line 196)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.color_of_ne_opposite`** (line 203)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.reply_no_loss_gen`** (line 209): ITEM 3 for w = 0: a reply of theirs that is neither a promotion nor (with no en-passant square) an en passant, from a position where nothing of ours (not the king) is attacked, loses us no material and gains them none
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.reply_no_loss`** (line 254): The same with the en-passant square gone (the case `hanging` has after a move that is not a pawn's).
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.move_is_normal`** (line 267): A legal move from a piece that is not a pawn onto a piece (so not onto an empty square) is a normal move.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.kingSq_board`** (line 295)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.attacked_withEmpty`** (line 309): Lifting our king off the board only adds to what they attack.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.reply_loses_at_most`** (line 350): **A REPLY LOSES AT MOST THE MOST VALUABLE PIECE ATTACKED** (plan 44): from a position `q` with them to move, any legal reply that is neither a promotion nor an en passant leaves their material no higher, and ours lower b
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.no_promotion`** (line 405): No pawn of theirs on the rank before promotion (our second rank, named from our side): they have no promotion.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.newEnPassant_nonpawn`** (line 423)
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.kingSq_exists`** (line 433): A board that holds side `c`'s king somewhere has a king square.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.kingSq_exists_of_not_inCheck`** (line 445): A position in which side `c` is not in check has a king of `c`: with none the kernel reads "in check".
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.safe_of_hit`** (line 453): WHAT `hit` = 0 GIVES: with our king (the board's own) lifted, none of our pieces is attacked; then none is attacked on the board as it is, since lifting a king only adds attacks.
- **theorem `Ideas.Root.no_enpassant`** (line 472): No en passant for them when they have no pawn on our fourth rank (named from `c`'s side): the pawn that takes en passant stands on its fifth rank, which is our fourth.

- **theorem `Ideas.Root.root_tail`** (line 608): THE TAIL OF A DELAYED ROOT (plan 44.2)

- **theorem `Ideas.Root.maxWorthOf_le_nine`** (line 690): No piece is worth more than a queen: `maxWorthOf` is at most 9.

- **theorem `Ideas.Root.root_tail_max_alive`** (line 828): `root_tail_max` with `alive` read from our material: the min-body (our material at least 13: they take at most one piece, worth at most 9), no `k ≥ 4`.

## lean/ideas/Ideas/Schema.lean

- **inductive `Ideas.Tier`** (line 20): `unconditional`: holds whatever the opponent does
- **structure `Ideas.Hyp`** (line 27): One hypothesis: a name the planner and the renderer can point at, and a decidable test.
- **structure `Ideas.Idea`** (line 33): An idea: parameters `P`, hypotheses, the piece it is about, tier, horizon in plies
- **def `Ideas.Idea.holds`** (line 45): Every hypothesis holds.
- **def `Ideas.Idea.failing`** (line 49): The hypotheses that fail, by name
- **def `Ideas.Idea.evalHyps`** (line 53): Each hypothesis with its value, in order (what the detector reports).
- **def `Ideas.Idea.evalUntil`** (line 59): The hypotheses in order, stopping after the second failure: the verdicts MATE (none fail) and ONEAWAY (exactly one fails) need every hypothesis; FAIL needs only two, so the expensive tests at the end of the list are skip
- **def `Ideas.sqOfIndex`** (line 74): The board read once into an array: the same function, constant-time lookups for the detector (FEN parsing builds it as 64 nested updates).
- **def `Ideas.boardOfArray`** (line 79): A board backed by a 64-entry array
- **def `Ideas.boardArray`** (line 86): The board's 64 squares, read once.
- **theorem `Ideas.boardArray_size`** (line 89)
- **theorem `Ideas.memoBoard_eq`** (line 95)

## lean/ideas/Main.lean

- **def `parseSquare`** (line 28): "d5" -> the square; none on anything else.
- **def `hexDigit`** (line 49): Parse a FEN and read its board into an array once (`memoBoard`, equal to the parsed board by `memoBoard_eq`; the array is bound here so every lookup shares it).
- **def `parseHex`** (line 56): A hex bitboard as a machine word (a `Nat` above 2^63 is a bignum; every bit test would allocate).
- **def `arrayOfBitboards`** (line 63): The 64 squares from python-chess bitboards (bit `rank * 8 + file`): pawns, knights, bishops, rooks, queens, kings, and the white occupancy.
- **theorem `arrayOfBitboards_size`** (line 73)
- **def `readBitboards`** (line 77): `bb <w|b> <castling|-> <ep|-> <pawns> <knights> <bishops> <rooks> <queens> <kings> <white>` in hex.
- **def `candidates`** (line 97): Candidate parameters: our rook or queen on `a`, a square `c` of the defender's home rank its geometry reaches on an empty board, the defender's king on `k` (on that rank).
  - used by: catspace/approaches/bootstrap_mate/src/engine.py, catspace/approaches/gauntlet_harness/experiments/play_traced.py, catspace/deployment/server/assistant_server.py, catspace/incubator/analyst/body_guide.py, catspace/incubator/analyst/body_guide_tf.py, catspace/incubator/analyst/body_search.py ...
- **def `passerCandidates`** (line 129): Passer candidates: every pawn of the side to move.
- **def `reportPasser2`** (line 152): Both forms are theorems, so a pawn is PROVED when either form's hypotheses all hold (the second form's room count over-approximates the passer's future attacks and loses some positions the first form keeps)
- **def `benchOne`** (line 251): Time `f` over `reps` runs; `f` gets the iteration index and the results are folded into a ref, so nothing is hoisted or elided.
- **def `bench`** (line 262): `bench <reps> <fen>`: each hypothesis of the first candidate, and the primitives, timed alone.
